Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Let the biological structure S satisfy the conditions of convergence analysis framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 570. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 22 and converges at rate O(1/n^2).

Proof: The convergence analysis estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 570 + O(1/n^22). Summing over all modes and applying the biological convergence analysis criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: The biological operator L associated with structural decomposition theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 613. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 752 with 2 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the structural decomposition decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_613(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 613. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^752(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 216.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any biological module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 636. The projective resolution has length at most 505 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: Consider the optimality condition operator L on the biological space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 636 for all k. By the optimality condition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 505. QED.
